    29→_DEFAULT_TEST_PDF = "data/protocols/crc_protocols/isrctn/48616-d8fc1476.pdf"
    30→
    31→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    32→# Stack availability detection
    33→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    34→
    35→_STACK_AVAILABLE: bool | None = None
    36→
    37→_DEFAULT_API_URL = "http://localhost:8000"
    38→_DEFAULT_DATABASE_URL = "postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:5432/app"
    39→
    40→
    41→def _check_stack() -> bool:
    42→    """Check whether the Docker Compose stack (API + PostgreSQL) is reachable."""
    43→    api_url = os.getenv("E2E_API_URL", _DEFAULT_API_URL)
    44→    try:
    45→        resp = httpx.get(f"{api_url}/health", timeout=3.0)
    46→        if resp.status_code != 200:
    47→            return False
    48→    except (httpx.ConnectError, httpx.TimeoutException, Exception):
    49→        return False
    50→
    51→    # Also verify PostgreSQL is reachable
    52→    try:
    53→        from sqlalchemy import create_engine, text
    54→
    55→        db_url = os.getenv("E2E_DATABASE_URL", _DEFAULT_DATABASE_URL)
    56→        engine = create_engine(db_url, connect_args={"connect_timeout": 3})
    57→        with engine.connect() as conn:
    58→            conn.execute(text("SELECT 1"))
    59→        engine.dispose()
    60→    except Exception:
    61→        return False
    62→
    63→    return True
    64→
    65→
    66→def _wants_e2e(config: pytest.Config) -> bool:
    67→    """Return True only if the user explicitly requested e2e tests.
    68→
    69→    This guard prevents the health-check from adding latency to regular
    70→    ``uv run pytest`` invocations that do not target E2E tests.
    71→    """
    72→    # Check -m flag for e2e marker expression
    73→    markexpr: str = config.getoption("-m", default="")
    74→    if markexpr and "e2e" in markexpr:
    75→        return True
    76→
    77→    # Check if any explicit test path is under tests/e2e/
    78→    args: list[str] = config.getoption("file_or_dir", default=[]) or []
    79→    for arg in args:
    80→        if "tests/e2e" in str(arg) or "tests\\e2e" in str(arg):
    81→            return True
    82→
    83→    return False
    84→
    85→
    86→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    87→# Pytest hooks
    88→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    89→
    90→
    91→def pytest_configure(config: pytest.Config) -> None:
    92→    """Detect Docker Compose availability once per session (if e2e requested)."""
    93→    global _STACK_AVAILABLE
    94→    if _wants_e2e(config):
    95→        _STACK_AVAILABLE = _check_stack()
    96→    else:
    97→        # Don't waste time checking -- no e2e tests requested
    98→        _STACK_AVAILABLE = False
    99→

   105→    if _STACK_AVAILABLE:

   392→def wait_for_pipeline(

   398→    """Poll protocol status until it reaches a terminal state.
   399→
   400→    Args:
   401→        api_client: Authenticated httpx client.
   402→        protocol_id: Protocol to monitor.
   403→        timeout: Maximum seconds to wait.
   404→        poll_interval: Seconds between polls.
   405→
   406→    Returns:
   407→        The final protocol response dict.
   408→
   409→    Raises:
   410→        TimeoutError: If timeout is exceeded before reaching terminal state.
   411→    """
   412→    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
   413→    while True:
   414→        resp = api_client.get(f"/protocols/{protocol_id}")
   415→        resp.raise_for_status()
   416→        data = resp.json()

   418→        status = data.get("status", "")

   422→                status,
   423→                protocol_id,

   426→
   427→        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
   428→            raise TimeoutError(
   429→                f"Protocol {protocol_id} still in '{status}' after {timeout}s"
   430→            )
   431→
   432→        time.sleep(poll_interval)
   433→
```

     4→  api:

```
     1→"""Full pipeline E2E tests: upload PDF -> extraction -> grounding -> criteria with entities.
     2→
     3→Tests cover:
     4→- E2E-01: Pipeline completes to pending_review status
     5→- E2E-02: Extracted criteria include both inclusion and exclusion types
     6→- E2E-03: Entities are grounded with non-zero confidence and coding references
     7→- E2E-06: Numeric regression baseline thresholds are met
     8→
     9→Each test uploads its own PDF (independent, no shared state). The cleanup
    10→fixture in conftest.py is autouse and handles deletion after each test.
    11→
    12→Requires:
    13→- Docker Compose stack running (API + PostgreSQL + protocol-processor)
    14→- Real Gemini API access (GOOGLE_API_KEY or ADC configured)
    15→- Test PDF at data/protocols/crc_protocols/isrctn/48616-d8fc1476.pdf
    16→"""
    17→
    18→from __future__ import annotations
    19→
    20→from typing import Callable
    21→
    22→import httpx
    23→import pytest
    24→
    25→from .baseline import get_baseline
    26→from .conftest import wait_for_pipeline
    27→
    28→# Default test PDF (smallest CRC protocol, ~90K)
    29→_DEFAULT_PDF = "data/protocols/crc_protocols/isrctn/48616-d8fc1476.pdf"
    30→
    31→_FAILURE_STATUSES = frozenset(
    32→    {"extraction_failed", "grounding_failed", "pipeline_failed", "dead_letter"}
    33→)
    34→
    35→
    36→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    37→# Shared helpers (module-level, not fixtures)
    38→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    39→
    40→
    41→def _upload_and_wait(
    42→    upload_test_pdf: Callable[..., str],
    43→    e2e_api_client: httpx.Client,
    44→    pdf_path: str | None = None,
    45→) -> tuple[str, dict]:
    46→    """Upload a PDF and wait for the pipeline to reach a terminal status.
    47→
    48→    Args:
    49→        upload_test_pdf: Factory fixture that uploads a PDF and returns protocol_id.
    50→        e2e_api_client: Authenticated httpx client.
    51→        pdf_path: Optional path to a specific PDF. Uses default if None.
    52→
    53→    Returns:
    54→        Tuple of (protocol_id, protocol_data dict).
    55→
    56→    Raises:
    57→        AssertionError: If pipeline reaches a failure status instead of pending_review.
    58→    """
    59→    protocol_id = upload_test_pdf(pdf_path=pdf_path or _DEFAULT_PDF)
    60→    protocol_data = wait_for_pipeline(e2e_api_client, protocol_id, timeout=180)
    61→
    62→    status = protocol_data.get("status", "")
    63→    assert status not in _FAILURE_STATUSES, (
    64→        f"Pipeline failed with status '{status}' for protocol {protocol_id}"
    65→    )
    66→    assert status == "pending_review", (
    67→        f"Expected 'pending_review' but got '{status}' for protocol {protocol_id}"
    68→    )
    69→
    70→    return protocol_id, protocol_data
    71→
    72→
    73→def _fetch_criteria(
    74→    e2e_api_client: httpx.Client,
    75→    protocol_id: str,
    76→) -> list[dict]:
    77→    """Fetch criteria for a protocol by retrieving its first batch.
    78→
    79→    Args:
    80→        e2e_api_client: Authenticated httpx client.
    81→        protocol_id: Protocol to fetch criteria for.
    82→
    83→    Returns:
    84→        List of criterion dicts (each with entities list).
    85→
    86→    Raises:
    87→        AssertionError: If no batches or no criteria found.
    88→    """
    89→    # Get batches for the protocol
    90→    batches_resp = e2e_api_client.get(f"/protocols/{protocol_id}/batches")
    91→    assert batches_resp.status_code == 200, (
    92→        f"Failed to fetch batches ({batches_resp.status_code}): {batches_resp.text}"
    93→    )
    94→    batches = batches_resp.json()
    95→    assert len(batches) > 0, f"No batches found for protocol {protocol_id}"
    96→
    97→    batch_id = batches[0]["id"]
    98→
    99→    # Get criteria for the first batch
   100→    criteria_resp = e2e_api_client.get(f"/reviews/batches/{batch_id}/criteria")
   101→    assert criteria_resp.status_code == 200, (
   102→        f"Failed to fetch criteria ({criteria_resp.status_code}): {criteria_resp.text}"
   103→    )
   104→    criteria = criteria_resp.json()
   105→    assert len(criteria) > 0, (
   106→        f"No criteria found in batch {batch_id} for protocol {protocol_id}"
   107→    )
   108→
   109→    return criteria
   110→
   111→
   112→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   113→# Test class
   114→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   115→
   116→
   117→@pytest.mark.e2e
   118→class TestFullPipeline:
   119→    """E2E tests for the full upload-to-criteria-with-entities pipeline."""
   120→
   121→    def test_pipeline_completes_successfully(
   122→        self,
   123→        upload_test_pdf: Callable[..., str],
   124→        e2e_api_client: httpx.Client,
   125→    ) -> None:
   126→        """E2E-01: Pipeline reaches pending_review status after uploading a real PDF."""
   127→        _protocol_id, protocol_data = _upload_and_wait(upload_test_pdf, e2e_api_client)
   128→        assert protocol_data["status"] == "pending_review"
   129→
   130→    def test_criteria_have_inclusion_and_exclusion(
   131→        self,
   132→        upload_test_pdf: Callable[..., str],
   133→        e2e_api_client: httpx.Client,
   134→    ) -> None:
   135→        """E2E-02: Extracted criteria include both inclusion and exclusion types."""
   136→        protocol_id, _data = _upload_and_wait(upload_test_pdf, e2e_api_client)
   137→        criteria = _fetch_criteria(e2e_api_client, protocol_id)
   138→
   139→        baseline = get_baseline(_DEFAULT_PDF)
   140→
   141→        # Collect criteria types
   142→        types = [c["criteria_type"] for c in criteria]
   143→        inclusion_count = sum(1 for t in types if t == "inclusion")
   144→        exclusion_count = sum(1 for t in types if t == "exclusion")
   145→
   146→        assert "inclusion" in types, (
   147→            f"No inclusion criteria found. Types present: {set(types)}"
   148→        )
   149→        assert "exclusion" in types, (
   150→            f"No exclusion criteria found. Types present: {set(types)}"
   151→        )
   152→        assert len(criteria) >= baseline["min_criteria"], (
   153→            f"Total criteria ({len(criteria)}) below baseline ({baseline['min_criteria']})"
   154→        )
   155→        assert inclusion_count >= baseline["min_inclusion"], (
   156→            f"Inclusion count ({inclusion_count}) below baseline ({baseline['min_inclusion']})"
   157→        )
   158→        assert exclusion_count >= baseline["min_exclusion"], (
   159→            f"Exclusion count ({exclusion_count}) below baseline ({baseline['min_exclusion']})"
   160→        )
   161→
   162→    def test_entities_grounded_with_confidence(
   163→        self,
   164→        upload_test_pdf: Callable[..., str],
   165→        e2e_api_client: httpx.Client,
   166→    ) -> None:
   167→        """E2E-03: Entities are grounded with non-zero confidence and coding refs."""
   168→        protocol_id, _data = _upload_and_wait(upload_test_pdf, e2e_api_client)
   169→        criteria = _fetch_criteria(e2e_api_client, protocol_id)
   170→
   171→        baseline = get_baseline(_DEFAULT_PDF)
   172→
   173→        # Collect all entities from all criteria
   174→        all_entities = []
   175→        for criterion in criteria:
   176→            all_entities.extend(criterion.get("entities", []))
   177→
   178→        assert len(all_entities) >= baseline["min_entities"], (
   179→            f"Total entities ({len(all_entities)}) below baseline ({baseline['min_entities']})"
   180→        )
   181→
   182→        # Filter grounded entities (non-null, non-zero confidence)
   183→        grounded = [
   184→            e
   185→            for e in all_entities
   186→            if e.get("grounding_confidence") is not None
   187→            and e["grounding_confidence"] > 0
   188→        ]
   189→        assert len(grounded) >= baseline["min_grounded_entities"], (
   190→            f"Grounded entities ({len(grounded)}) below baseline "
   191→            f"({baseline['min_grounded_entities']})"
   192→        )
   193→
   194→        # At least one grounded entity should have a coding reference
   195→        has_coding_ref = any(
   196→            e.get("umls_cui") is not None or e.get("snomed_code") is not None
   197→            for e in grounded
   198→        )
   199→        assert has_coding_ref, (
   200→            "No grounded entity has a non-null umls_cui or snomed_code"
   201→        )
   202→
   203→    def test_regression_baseline(
   204→        self,
   205→        upload_test_pdf: Callable[..., str],
   206→        e2e_api_client: httpx.Client,
   207→    ) -> None:
   208→        """E2E-06: All numeric regression baseline thresholds are met."""
   209→        protocol_id, _data = _upload_and_wait(upload_test_pdf, e2e_api_client)
   210→        criteria = _fetch_criteria(e2e_api_client, protocol_id)
   211→
   212→        baseline = get_baseline(_DEFAULT_PDF)
   213→
   214→        # Compute actual counts
   215→        types = [c["criteria_type"] for c in criteria]
   216→        inclusion_count = sum(1 for t in types if t == "inclusion")
   217→        exclusion_count = sum(1 for t in types if t == "exclusion")
   218→
   219→        all_entities = []
   220→        for criterion in criteria:
   221→            all_entities.extend(criterion.get("entities", []))
   222→
   223→        grounded = [
   224→            e
   225→            for e in all_entities
   226→            if e.get("grounding_confidence") is not None
   227→            and e["grounding_confidence"] > 0
   228→        ]
   229→
   230→        # Print actual vs baseline for visibility (pytest -s)
   231→        print(f"\n--- Regression Baseline Report for {_DEFAULT_PDF} ---")
   232→        print(
   233→            f"  Criteria:          {len(criteria):>4} (baseline: {baseline['min_criteria']})"
   234→        )
   235→        print(
   236→            f"  Inclusion:         {inclusion_count:>4} (baseline: {baseline['min_inclusion']})"
   237→        )
   238→        print(
   239→            f"  Exclusion:         {exclusion_count:>4} (baseline: {baseline['min_exclusion']})"
   240→        )
   241→        print(
   242→            f"  Entities:          {len(all_entities):>4} (baseline: {baseline['min_entities']})"
   243→        )
   244→        print(
   245→            f"  Grounded entities: {len(grounded):>4} (baseline: {baseline['min_grounded_entities']})"
   246→        )
   247→        print("---")
   248→
   249→        # Assert ALL baseline thresholds
   250→        assert len(criteria) >= baseline["min_criteria"], (
   251→            f"Total criteria ({len(criteria)}) below baseline ({baseline['min_criteria']})"
   252→        )
   253→        assert inclusion_count >= baseline["min_inclusion"], (
   254→            f"Inclusion count ({inclusion_count}) below baseline ({baseline['min_inclusion']})"
   255→        )
   256→        assert exclusion_count >= baseline["min_exclusion"], (
   257→            f"Exclusion count ({exclusion_count}) below baseline ({baseline['min_exclusion']})"
   258→        )
   259→        assert len(all_entities) >= baseline["min_entities"], (
   260→            f"Entity count ({len(all_entities)}) below baseline ({baseline['min_entities']})"
   261→        )
   262→        assert len(grounded) >= baseline["min_grounded_entities"], (
   263→            f"Grounded entity count ({len(grounded)}) below baseline "
   264→            f"({baseline['min_grounded_entities']})"
   265→        )
   266→

## Complete LangGraph Pipeline Smoketest Guide

Based on my analysis of the codebase, here's everything you need to invoke the LangGraph pipeline directly:

---

### **1. PIPELINE STATE SCHEMA** (exact definition)

Location: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/state.py`

```python
from typing_extensions import TypedDict
from typing import Literal

class PipelineState(TypedDict):
    """Minimal state for protocol processing pipeline."""
    
    # Input (always present)
    protocol_id: str
    file_uri: str
    title: str
    
    # Created by parse node
    batch_id: str | None
    
    # Processing artifacts (populated on-demand)
    pdf_bytes: bytes | None                    # Populated by ingest
    extraction_json: str | None                # Populated by extract (JSON string)
    entities_json: str | None                  # Populated by parse (JSON string)
    grounded_entities_json: str | None         # Populated by ground (JSON string)
    
    # Re-extraction context (optional)
    archived_reviewed_criteria: list[dict] | None
    
    # Ordinal resolution
    ordinal_proposals_json: str | None
    
    # Output
    status: Literal["processing", "completed", "failed"]
    error: str | None
    errors: list[str]
```

---

### **2. DATABASE SETUP** 

**Connection string:** PostgreSQL on `localhost:5432`, database `app`
```python
DATABASE_URL=postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:5432/app
```

**Models to import:**
```python
from shared.models import Protocol, CriteriaBatch, Criteria, Entity
from api_service.storage import engine
from sqlmodel import Session
```

**Create test Protocol record:**
```python
from uuid import uuid4

protocol = Protocol(
    id=str(uuid4()),
    title="Test Clinical Trial Protocol",
    file_uri="local://path/to/test.pdf",  # or gs://bucket/path
    status="uploaded",
    page_count=None,
    quality_score=None,
    error_reason=None,
    metadata_={},
)

with Session(engine) as session:
    session.add(protocol)
    session.commit()
    protocol_id = protocol.id
```

---

### **3. GRAPH INVOCATION PATTERN**

The graph is **async-only**. Use this pattern:

```python
import asyncio
from uuid import uuid4
from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph
from protocol_processor.state import PipelineState

async def run_pipeline_smoketest():
    # Get compiled graph with checkpointer
    graph = await get_graph()
    
    # Build initial state
    initial_state: PipelineState = {
        "protocol_id": "your-protocol-id-here",
        "file_uri": "local://path/to/test.pdf",
        "title": "Test Protocol",
        "batch_id": None,
        "pdf_bytes": None,
        "extraction_json": None,
        "entities_json": None,
        "grounded_entities_json": None,
        "archived_reviewed_criteria": None,
        "ordinal_proposals_json": None,
        "status": "processing",
        "error": None,
        "errors": [],
    }
    
    # Configure with unique thread_id (for checkpointing)
    config = {
        "configurable": {
            "thread_id": f"{protocol_id}:{uuid4()}"
        }
    }
    
    # Invoke the graph
    result = await graph.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
    return result

# Entry point
result = asyncio.run(run_pipeline_smoketest())
```

---

### **4. CRITICAL IMPORTS FOR YOUR SCRIPT**

---

### **5. FULL SMOKETEST SCRIPT STRUCTURE**

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Smoketest for LangGraph pipeline invocation."""

import asyncio
import json
import os
from uuid import uuid4
import pathlib

from api_service.storage import engine, create_db_and_tables
from shared.models import Protocol, CriteriaBatch, Criteria, Entity
from sqlmodel import Session
from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph
from protocol_processor.state import PipelineState


async def setup_test_protocol(pdf_path: str) -> str:
    """Create a test Protocol record and return its ID."""
    # Create tables if they don't exist
    create_db_and_tables()
    
    # Create protocol
    protocol = Protocol(
        id=str(uuid4()),
        title="Smoketest Protocol: Type 2 Diabetes",
        file_uri=f"local://{pathlib.Path(pdf_path).absolute()}",
        status="uploaded",
        metadata_={},
    )
    
    with Session(engine) as session:
        session.add(protocol)
        session.commit()
        return protocol.id


async def run_smoketest(protocol_id: str) -> dict:
    """Invoke the pipeline directly."""
    
    # Build initial state
    initial_state: PipelineState = {
        "protocol_id": protocol_id,
        "file_uri": f"local://path/to/test.pdf",
        "title": "Smoketest Protocol",
        "batch_id": None,
        "pdf_bytes": None,
        "extraction_json": None,
        "entities_json": None,
        "grounded_entities_json": None,
        "archived_reviewed_criteria": None,
        "ordinal_proposals_json": None,
        "status": "processing",
        "error": None,
        "errors": [],
    }
    
    # Get graph
    graph = await get_graph()
    
    # Invoke with checkpointing
    config = {
        "configurable": {
            "thread_id": f"{protocol_id}:{uuid4()}"
        }
    }
    
    result = await graph.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
    return result


async def check_results(protocol_id: str) -> None:
    """Verify pipeline results in the database."""
    
    with Session(engine) as session:
        # Fetch protocol
        protocol = session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
        if not protocol:
            print("ERROR: Protocol not found")
            return
        
        print(f"\nProtocol Status: {protocol.status}")
        print(f"Error Reason: {protocol.error_reason}")
        
        # Fetch CriteriaBatch
        from sqlmodel import select
        
        stmt = select(CriteriaBatch).where(
            CriteriaBatch.protocol_id == protocol_id
        )
        batch = session.exec(stmt).first()
        
        if batch:
            print(f"\nCriteriaBatch ID: {batch.id}")
            print(f"Batch Status: {batch.status}")
            
            # Fetch Criteria
            criteria_stmt = select(Criteria).where(
                Criteria.batch_id == batch.id
            )
            criteria_list = session.exec(criteria_stmt).all()
            print(f"Criteria Count: {len(criteria_list)}")
            
            # Fetch Entities
            if criteria_list:
                criterion_ids = [c.id for c in criteria_list]
                entities_stmt = select(Entity).where(
                    Entity.criteria_id.in_(criterion_ids)
                )
                entities = session.exec(entities_stmt).all()
                print(f"Entity Count: {len(entities)}")
                
                # Show groundings
                for entity in entities[:3]:  # First 3 entities
                    print(f"\n  Entity: {entity.text}")
                    print(f"  Type: {entity.entity_type}")
                    print(f"  SNOMED Code: {entity.snomed_code}")
                    print(f"  UMLS CUI: {entity.umls_cui}")
                    print(f"  Confidence: {entity.grounding_confidence}")
        else:
            print("No CriteriaBatch created (extraction may have failed)")


async def main():
    """Main smoketest entry point."""
    
    # Set up environment
    if not os.getenv("DATABASE_URL"):
        os.environ["DATABASE_URL"] = "postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:5432/app"
    
    print("=" * 60)
    print("LangGraph Pipeline Smoketest")
    print("=" * 60)
    
    # 1. Create test protocol
    print("\n[1] Creating test protocol...")
    protocol_id = await setup_test_protocol("/path/to/your/test.pdf")
    print(f"    Protocol ID: {protocol_id}")
    
    # 2. Run pipeline
    print("\n[2] Invoking LangGraph pipeline...")
    try:
        result = await run_smoketest(protocol_id)
        print(f"    Pipeline Status: {result.get('status')}")
        if result.get('error'):
            print(f"    Pipeline Error: {result.get('error')}")
        if result.get('errors'):
            print(f"    Partial Errors: {len(result.get('errors', []))} errors")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"    ERROR: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
    
    # 3. Check results
    print("\n[3] Checking database results...")
    await check_results(protocol_id)
    
    print("\n" + "=" * 60)
    print("Smoketest Complete")
    print("=" * 60)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    asyncio.run(main())
```

### **6. ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES REQUIRED**

export DATABASE_URL=postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:5432/app

### **7. RUNNING THE SMOKETEST**

# Or with explicit environment setup

uv run python services/protocol-processor-service/scripts/smoketest.py
```

---

### **8. KEY PIPELINE NODES & FLOW**

The 7-node graph runs in this order:

1. **ingest_node** — Fetches PDF bytes from file_uri
2. **extract_node** — Calls Gemini to extract criteria (returns JSON string)
3. **parse_node** — Creates CriteriaBatch + Criteria records, decomposes entities
4. **ground_node** — Calls UMLS/MedGemma to ground entities to medical codes
5. **persist_node** — Creates Entity records, updates protocol status
6. **structure_node** — Builds expression trees (optional phase)
7. **ordinal_resolve_node** — Resolves ordinal scales (optional phase)

**Error handling:** If error is set after ingest/extract/parse, graph routes to END. Ground and persist use error accumulation (partial success allowed).

---

### **9. STATE DIAGRAM**

```
PipelineState
├─ input_phase (ingest)
│  └─ pdf_bytes: bytes (128KB-50MB PDFs)
│
├─ extraction_phase (extract → parse)
│  ├─ extraction_json: JSON string (from Gemini)
│  └─ entities_json: JSON string (decomposed entities)
│
├─ grounding_phase (ground → persist)
│  └─ grounded_entities_json: JSON string (UMLS results)
│
├─ persistence (persist)
│  └─ Creates Entity records in DB
│
└─ output_phase
   ├─ status: "processing" → "completed" or "failed"
   ├─ error: None or fatal error message
   └─ errors: list of partial failure messages
```

---

### **10. EXPECTED DATABASE OUTPUT**

After successful pipeline:

```
Protocol
  ├─ status: "pending_review" (or "grounding_failed" on errors)
  └─ metadata_: {"pipeline_thread_id": "..."}

CriteriaBatch (1 per protocol)
  ├─ id: UUID
  ├─ protocol_id: UUID
  └─ status: "pending_review"

Criteria (multiple per batch)
  ├─ batch_id: UUID
  ├─ text: "Patient must be 18-85 years old"
  ├─ criteria_type: "inclusion" or "exclusion"
  └─ conditions: {"field_mappings": [...]}

Entity (multiple per criterion)
  ├─ criteria_id: UUID
  ├─ text: "18-85 years"
  ├─ entity_type: "Demographic"
  ├─ snomed_code: "12345..."
  ├─ umls_cui: "C0001..."
  └─ grounding_confidence: 0.95
```

---

| **Graph import** | `from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph` |

| **Config dict** | `{"configurable": {"thread_id": "protocol_id:uuid4"}}` |
| **Database engine** | `from api_service.storage import engine` |

| **Entry point** | `asyncio.run(async_function())` |
| **File lookup** | `local://` prefix for local paths, `gs://` for GCS |

This gives you everything needed to bypass the API and invoke the pipeline directly!

Perfect! Now I have a clear picture. Let me create a summary of my findings:

## Summary: API Service Startup Issue Analysis

I've identified the root cause of the "relation auditlog already exists" error. Here's what I found:

### 1. **Where the Startup Happens** (`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/api-service/src/api_service/main.py`)
   - Line 50: `create_db_and_tables()` is called during the FastAPI lifespan startup
   - This happens BEFORE migrations are run in the Docker startup sequence

### 2. **The Root Cause - Dual Table Creation**

The problem is a **double initialization of the `auditlog` table**:

**a) In `storage.py` (line 22-24):**
```python
def create_db_and_tables() -> None:
    """Create all database tables."""
    SQLModel.metadata.create_all(engine)
```

This uses SQLModel to create all tables defined in the models, including `AuditLog` from `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/libs/shared/src/shared/models.py` (line 158-167).

**b) In Alembic migration** (`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/api-service/alembic/versions/91004fffe85d_initial_domain_models.py`, line 26-41):

The initial migration explicitly creates the `auditlog` table again via SQL.

### 3. **Dockerfile Startup Sequence** (`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/api-service/Dockerfile`)

Line 29:
```bash
CMD ["sh", "-c", "cd services/api-service && uv run --no-sync alembic upgrade head && uv run --no-sync uvicorn api_service.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000"]
```

The execution order is:
1. **Alembic runs first**: `alembic upgrade head` creates tables including `auditlog`
2. **FastAPI starts**: When the app starts, `create_db_and_tables()` tries to create ALL tables again
3. **Error occurs**: PostgreSQL throws "relation auditlog already exists"

### 4. **Alembic Configuration** (`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/api-service/alembic/env.py`)

Lines 7-14 import all models:
```python
from shared.models import (
    AuditLog,
    Criteria,
    CriteriaBatch,
    Entity,
    OutboxEvent,
    Protocol,
    Review,
)
```

These are registered with SQLModel.metadata, which is used in both the migration (line 35) and the `create_db_and_tables()` function.

### **Solution**

The issue is that `create_db_and_tables()` should NOT be called when migrations are already handled by Alembic. You have two options:

1. **Remove `create_db_and_tables()` call** from `main.py` line 50 (since Alembic handles it)
2. **OR** move the `alembic upgrade head` to happen only on first deployment, and keep `create_db_and_tables()` for development/testing

The cleanest approach is typically **Option 1** - let Alembic be the source of truth for schema management in production.

    91→    task = asyncio.create_task(processor.start())
    92→    _running_tasks.add(task)
    93→    task.add_done_callback(_running_tasks.discard)
    94→
    95→    yield
    96→
    97→    # Shutdown - stop outbox processor first
    98→    await processor.stop()
    99→
   100→    # Wait for background tasks to complete
   101→    if _running_tasks:
   102→        logger.info(f"Waiting for {len(_running_tasks)} tasks to complete...")
   103→        await asyncio.gather(*_running_tasks, return_exceptions=True)
   104→
   105→    engine.dispose()
   106→    logger.info("Shutdown complete")
   107→
   108→
   109→app = FastAPI(lifespan=lifespan)
   110→
   111→# Add SessionMiddleware for OAuth state (must be before CORS)
   112→_DEFAULT_SESSION_SECRET=[REDACTED]"
   113→_session_secret = os.getenv("SESSION_SECRET", _DEFAULT_SESSION_SECRET)
   114→_is_production = os.getenv("ENVIRONMENT", "").lower() == "production"
   115→if _is_production and _session_secret == _DEFAULT_SESSION_SECRET:
   116→    raise RuntimeError(
   117→        "SESSION_SECRET must be set to a secure value in production. "
   118→        "The default dev secret is not allowed."
   119→    )
   120→app.add_middleware(
   121→    SessionMiddleware,
   122→    secret_key=_session_secret,
   123→)
   124→
   125→# Configure CORS
   126→# In production, restrict origins to specific domains
   127→cors_origins = os.getenv(
   128→    "CORS_ORIGINS",
   129→    "http://localhost:3000,http://localhost:3001,http://localhost:3002,http://localhost:3003,http://localhost:5173,http://localhost:5174",
   130→).split(",")
   131→
   132→app.add_middleware(
   133→    CORSMiddleware,
   134→    allow_origins=cors_origins,
   135→    allow_credentials=True,
   136→    allow_methods=["*"],
   137→    allow_headers=["*"],
   138→)
   139→
   140→# Add MLflow request tracing middleware
   141→app.add_middleware(MLflowRequestMiddleware)
   142→
   143→
   144→@app.exception_handler(Exception)
   145→async def global_exception_handler(request: Request, exc: Exception) -> JSONResponse:
   146→    """Catch unhandled exceptions and return JSON so CORS headers are preserved."""
   147→    logger.error(
   148→        "Unhandled exception on %s %s: %s", request.method, request.url.path, exc
   149→    )
   150→    return JSONResponse(
   151→        status_code=500,
   152→        content={"detail": "Internal server error"},
   153→    )
   154→
   155→
   156→# Mount auth router (public endpoints - no auth required)
   157→app.include_router(auth_router)
   158→
   159→# Mount protected routers (all endpoints require auth)
   160→app.include_router(protocols_router, dependencies=[Depends(get_current_user)])
   161→app.include_router(reviews_router, dependencies=[Depends(get_current_user)])
   162→app.include_router(entities_router, dependencies=[Depends(get_current_user)])
   163→app.include_router(search_router, dependencies=[Depends(get_current_user)])
   164→app.include_router(terminology_search_router, dependencies=[Depends(get_current_user)])
   165→app.include_router(integrity_router, dependencies=[Depends(get_current_user)])
   166→app.include_router(criterion_rerun_router, dependencies=[Depends(get_current_user)])
   167→app.include_router(batch_compare_router, dependencies=[Depends(get_current_user)])
   168→app.include_router(exports_router, dependencies=[Depends(get_current_user)])
   169→
   170→
   171→@app.get("/health")
   172→async def health_check():
   173→    """Liveness probe - is the service running?"""
   174→    return {"status": "healthy"}
   175→
   176→
   177→@app.get("/ready")
   178→async def readiness_check(db: Session = Depends(get_db)):
   179→    """Readiness probe - can the service handle requests?"""
   180→    try:
   181→        # Test database connection
   182→        db.execute(text("SELECT 1"))
   183→        return {"status": "ready", "database": "connected"}
   184→    except Exception as e:
   185→        logger.error(f"Readiness check failed: {e}")
   186→        return JSONResponse(
   187→            status_code=503,
   188→            content={
   189→                "status": "not_ready",
   190→                "error": "Database unavailable",
   191→            },
   192→        )
   193→
   194→
   195→@app.get("/")
   196→async def root():
   197→    """Returns a welcome message for the API."""
   198→    return {"message": "Welcome to the API Service"}
   199→
   200→
   201→# --- Local file storage endpoints (dev only) ---
   202→
   203→
   204→@app.put("/local-upload/{blob_path:path}")
   205→async def local_upload(blob_path: str, request: Request):
   206→    """Receive a file upload and store it locally (dev mode)."""
   207→    from api_service.gcs import local_save_file
   208→
   209→    body = await request.body()
   210→    local_save_file(blob_path, body)
   211→    return JSONResponse(status_code=200, content={"status": "ok"})
   212→
   213→
   214→@app.get("/local-files/{blob_path:path}")
   215→async def local_files(blob_path: str):
   216→    """Serve a locally stored file (dev mode)."""
   217→    from api_service.gcs import local_get_file_path
   218→
   219→    file_path = local_get_file_path(blob_path)
   220→    if file_path is None:
   221→        return JSONResponse(status_code=404, content={"detail": "File not found"})
   222→    return FileResponse(str(file_path), media_type="application/pdf")
   223→

{
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/scripts/e2e_smoketest.py",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"E2E smoketest: upload a PDF through the API and wait for pipeline completion.\n\nUsage:\n    uv run python scripts/e2e_smoketest.py [path/to/test.pdf]\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport base64\nimport json\nimport sys\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport httpx\nimport jwt\n\nAPI_URL = \"http://localhost:8000\"\nJWT_SECRET = \"dev-secret-key-change-in-production\"\nDEFAULT_PDF = \"data/protocols/clinicaltrials/Prot_000-f1ed5129.pdf\"\nTIMEOUT_SECONDS = 300\nPOLL_INTERVAL = 5\n\nTERMINAL_STATUSES = frozenset(\n    {\n        \"pending_review\",\n        \"complete\",\n        \"extraction_failed\",\n        \"grounding_failed\",\n        \"pipeline_failed\",\n        \"dead_letter\",\n    }\n)\n\n\ndef make_client() -> httpx.Client:\n    token = jwt.encode(\n        {\"sub\": \"smoketest-user\", \"email\": \"smoketest@test.com\", \"name\": \"Smoketest\"},\n        JWT_SECRET,\n        algorithm=\"HS256\",\n    )\n    return httpx.Client(\n        base_url=API_URL,\n        headers={\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {token}\"},\n        timeout=60.0,\n    )\n\n\ndef upload_pdf(client: httpx.Client, pdf_path: Path) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Upload a PDF through the 3-step API flow. Returns protocol_id.\"\"\"\n    pdf_bytes = pdf_path.read_bytes()\n    print(f\"  PDF size: {len(pdf_bytes):,} bytes\")\n\n    # Step 1: Request upload URL\n    resp = client.post(\n        \"/protocols/upload\",\n        json={\n            \"filename\": pdf_path.name,\n            \"content_type\": \"application/pdf\",\n            \"file_size_bytes\": len(pdf_bytes),\n        },\n    )\n    assert resp.status_code == 200, f\"Upload request failed ({resp.status_code}): {resp.text}\"\n    data = resp.json()\n    protocol_id = data[\"protocol_id\"]\n    upload_url = data[\"upload_url\"]\n    print(f\"  Protocol ID: {protocol_id}\")\n    print(f\"  Upload URL: {upload_url}\")\n\n    # Step 2: PUT the PDF bytes\n    put_resp = httpx.put(\n        upload_url,\n        content=pdf_bytes,\n        headers={\"Content-Type\": \"application/pdf\"},\n        timeout=30.0,\n    )\n    assert put_resp.status_code == 200, f\"PUT failed ({put_resp.status_code}): {put_resp.text}\"\n    print(\"  PUT upload: OK\")\n\n    # Step 3: Confirm upload\n    pdf_b64 = base64.b64encode(pdf_bytes).decode(\"ascii\")\n    confirm_resp = client.post(\n        f\"/protocols/{protocol_id}/confirm-upload\",\n        json={\"pdf_bytes_base64\": pdf_b64},\n    )\n    assert confirm_resp.status_code == 200, (\n        f\"Confirm failed ({confirm_resp.status_code}): {confirm_resp.text}\"\n    )\n    print(\"  Confirm upload: OK\")\n    return protocol_id\n\n\ndef wait_for_pipeline(client: httpx.Client, protocol_id: str) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Poll until the pipeline reaches a terminal status.\"\"\"\n    deadline = time.monotonic() + TIMEOUT_SECONDS\n    last_status = \"\"\n    while True:\n        resp = client.get(f\"/protocols/{protocol_id}\")\n        resp.raise_for_status()\n        data = resp.json()\n        status = data.get(\"status\", \"unknown\")\n\n        if status != last_status:\n            elapsed = TIMEOUT_SECONDS - (deadline - time.monotonic())\n            print(f\"  [{elapsed:5.0f}s] Status: {status}\")\n            last_status = status\n\n        if status in TERMINAL_STATUSES:\n            return data\n\n        if time.monotonic() > deadline:\n            raise TimeoutError(\n                f\"Protocol {protocol_id} still '{status}' after {TIMEOUT_SECONDS}s\"\n            )\n        time.sleep(POLL_INTERVAL)\n\n\ndef fetch_criteria(client: httpx.Client, protocol_id: str) -> list[dict]:\n    \"\"\"Fetch criteria from the first batch.\"\"\"\n    batches_resp = client.get(f\"/protocols/{protocol_id}/batches\")\n    batches_resp.raise_for_status()\n    batches = batches_resp.json()\n    if not batches:\n        print(\"  WARNING: No batches found\")\n        return []\n\n    batch_id = batches[0][\"id\"]\n    criteria_resp = client.get(f\"/reviews/batches/{batch_id}/criteria\")\n    criteria_resp.raise_for_status()\n    return criteria_resp.json()\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    repo_root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]\n    pdf_arg = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else DEFAULT_PDF\n    pdf_path = Path(pdf_arg)\n    if not pdf_path.is_absolute():\n        pdf_path = repo_root / pdf_path\n\n    assert pdf_path.exists(), f\"PDF not found: {pdf_path}\"\n\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"E2E Pipeline Smoketest\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    client = make_client()\n\n    # Health check\n    try:\n        health = client.get(\"/health\")\n        assert health.status_code == 200\n        print(\"\\n[1/4] API health check: OK\")\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"\\nERROR: API not reachable at {API_URL}: {e}\")\n        sys.exit(1)\n\n    # Upload\n    print(f\"\\n[2/4] Uploading PDF: {pdf_path.name}\")\n    start = time.monotonic()\n    protocol_id = upload_pdf(client, pdf_path)\n\n    # Wait for pipeline\n    print(f\"\\n[3/4] Waiting for pipeline (timeout={TIMEOUT_SECONDS}s)...\")\n    try:\n        result = wait_for_pipeline(client, protocol_id)\n    except TimeoutError as e:\n        print(f\"\\n  TIMEOUT: {e}\")\n        sys.exit(1)\n\n    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start\n    status = result.get(\"status\", \"unknown\")\n    print(f\"\\n  Pipeline finished in {elapsed:.1f}s with status: {status}\")\n\n    if status in {\"extraction_failed\", \"grounding_failed\", \"pipeline_failed\", \"dead_letter\"}:\n        print(f\"  ERROR REASON: {result.get('error_reason', 'unknown')}\")\n        sys.exit(1)\n\n    # Fetch and report criteria\n    print(f\"\\n[4/4] Fetching criteria...\")\n    criteria = fetch_criteria(client, protocol_id)\n    if not criteria:\n        print(\"  No criteria returned!\")\n        sys.exit(1)\n\n    types = [c[\"criteria_type\"] for c in criteria]\n    inclusion_count = sum(1 for t in types if t == \"inclusion\")\n    exclusion_count = sum(1 for t in types if t == \"exclusion\")\n\n    all_entities = []\n    for c in criteria:\n        all_entities.extend(c.get(\"entities\", []))\n\n    grounded = [\n        e for e in all_entities\n        if e.get(\"grounding_confidence\") is not None and e[\"grounding_confidence\"] > 0\n    ]\n\n    print(f\"\\n  --- Results ---\")\n    print(f\"  Total criteria:    {len(criteria)}\")\n    print(f\"  Inclusion:         {inclusion_count}\")\n    print(f\"  Exclusion:         {exclusion_count}\")\n    print(f\"  Total entities:    {len(all_entities)}\")\n    print(f\"  Grounded entities: {len(grounded)}\")\n    print(f\"  Pipeline time:     {elapsed:.1f}s\")\n\n    # Show a few sample entities\n    if grounded:\n        print(f\"\\n  --- Sample Grounded Entities (up to 5) ---\")\n        for e in grounded[:5]:\n            print(f\"    {e.get('text', '?')[:60]}\")\n            print(f\"      type={e.get('entity_type')} confidence={e.get('grounding_confidence')}\")\n            print(f\"      snomed={e.get('snomed_code')} umls={e.get('umls_cui')}\")\n\n    print(f\"\\n{'=' * 60}\")\n    print(\"SMOKETEST PASSED\" if status == \"pending_review\" else f\"SMOKETEST RESULT: {status}\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    client.close()\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
}

```
Now I've identified the critical bug. Let me create a comprehensive report:

## Root Cause Analysis: OutboxProcessor Not Processing Events

I've found the **critical bug** in the OutboxProcessor that causes events to remain stuck in "pending" status. Here's the issue:

### The Bug (in `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/libs/events-py/src/events_py/outbox.py`)

**Lines 88-104 have a logic flow problem:**

```python
for event in events:
    event_handlers = self.handlers.get(event.event_type, [])

    if not event_handlers:
        logger.debug(
            "No handlers for event type: %s",
            event.event_type,
        )

    try:
        for handler in event_handlers:
            handler(event.payload)

        event.status = "published"
        event.published_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
        session.add(event)
        processed += 1
        # ... logging ...
    except Exception:
        # handle errors ...
```

**The Problem:**

When there are **no handlers for an event type** (lines 91-95), the code:
1. Logs a debug message
2. **Continues directly to the `try` block** (line 97)
3. The `for handler in event_handlers:` loop runs 0 times (since `event_handlers` is empty)
4. The event status is set to "published" anyway (line 101)
5. The event is committed as published, even though no handler was registered

**BUT** — looking at your setup in `main.py`, line 88, the handlers dict correctly maps `"protocol_uploaded"` to `[handle_protocol_uploaded]`. However, there's a **second critical issue**:

### The Real Problem: Missing Handler Execution but Status Still Updates

The code does **not skip processing** when handlers are empty — it silently marks events as published without executing anything. However, this is actually working as designed (events with no handlers are marked published to clear them).

### The Actual Root Cause: Check Your Handler Registration

The real issue is likely **one of these scenarios**:

1. **Event Type Mismatch**: The event is being created with a different event_type than `"protocol_uploaded"`. Check:
   - In `protocols.py` line 260, what is `DomainEventKind.PROTOCOL_UPLOADED.value`?
   - Does it match exactly `"protocol_uploaded"` (all lowercase)?
   - The enum in `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/libs/events-py/src/events_py/models.py` line 22 defines it as `PROTOCOL_UPLOADED = "protocol_uploaded"` ✓ (correct)

2. **Silent Handler Failure**: The handler `handle_protocol_uploaded` (in `trigger.py`) might be:
   - Failing at line 279: `asyncio.run(_run_pipeline(...))`
   - Throwing an exception that gets caught but the event stays "pending" due to retry logic
   - The exception is caught and the event marked as "failed" (line 138), but never retried

3. **Processor Not Running**: The `asyncio.create_task(processor.start())` in `main.py` line 91:
   - May not actually be starting (check if the task is being added to `_running_tasks` correctly)
   - The event loop might not be running the background task
   - Check logs for "Outbox processor started" message

### Secondary Issue Found: **No Handler Execution on Empty Handlers**

At lines 91-99, when `event_handlers` is empty:
- The debug log is printed
- The code **doesn't skip the try block**
- An empty loop executes (does nothing)
- Event is marked published anyway

**This should probably be:**
```python
if not event_handlers:
    logger.debug("No handlers for event type: %s", event.event_type)
    # Mark as published to avoid reprocessing
    event.status = "published"
    event.published_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    session.add(event)
    processed += 1
    continue  # <-- SKIP THE TRY BLOCK
```

But this is only a secondary issue if you have events with unregistered types.

### Investigation Steps:

1. **Check the logs** for these patterns:
   - "Outbox processor started" — confirms processor is running
   - "No handlers for event type" — indicates event_type mismatch
   - "Failed to process event" — indicates handler exception
   - Check the event_type value being logged

2. **Verify event creation** in `protocols.py`:
   - Line 260: `DomainEventKind.PROTOCOL_UPLOADED` 
   - Confirm its `.value` is exactly `"protocol_uploaded"`

3. **Check handler registration** in `main.py` line 88:
   - Ensure the key is exactly `"protocol_uploaded"` (lowercase, no spaces)

4. **Check if events are being created at all**:
   - Query the OutboxEvent table to see if rows exist with status="pending"
   - Check their `event_type` value

5. **Monitor handler execution**:
   - Add logging to `handle_protocol_uploaded` start (line 233-237)
   - Check if exceptions are being silently swallowed

The most likely culprit is either an **event_type mismatch** or **handler exceptions being silently caught** by the exception handler at line 111.

     1→"""Outbox processor for publishing pending domain events.
     2→
     3→Implements the transactional outbox pattern with at-least-once delivery.
     4→The OutboxProcessor polls the outbox table for pending events and dispatches
     5→them to registered handlers. The persist_with_outbox helper enables atomic
     6→entity + event writes in a single database transaction.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import asyncio
    12→import contextvars
    13→import logging
    14→from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
    15→from datetime import datetime, timezone
    16→from typing import Any, Callable
    17→from uuid import uuid4
    18→
    19→from shared.models import OutboxEvent
    20→from sqlalchemy import Engine
    21→from sqlmodel import Session, col, select
    22→
    23→from events_py.models import DomainEventKind
    24→
    25→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    26→
    27→MAX_RETRIES = 3
    28→
    29→
    30→class OutboxProcessor:
    31→    """Processes pending outbox events with at-least-once delivery.
    32→
    33→    Usage:
    34→        processor = OutboxProcessor(engine, handlers={
    35→            "protocol_uploaded": [handle_protocol_uploaded],
    36→        })
    37→        await processor.start()  # Runs polling loop
    38→        await processor.stop()   # Graceful shutdown
    39→    """
    40→
    41→    def __init__(
    42→        self,
    43→        engine: Engine,
    44→        handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] | None = None,
    45→        poll_interval: float = 1.0,
    46→        batch_size: int = 100,
    47→    ) -> None:
    48→        """Initialize the outbox processor.
    49→
    50→        Args:
    51→            engine: SQLAlchemy database engine.
    52→            handlers: Maps event_type string to handler callbacks.
    53→            poll_interval: Seconds between polls.
    54→            batch_size: Max events per poll cycle.
    55→        """
    56→        self.engine = engine
    57→        self.handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] = handlers or {}
    58→        self.poll_interval = poll_interval
    59→        self.batch_size = batch_size
    60→        # Dedicated thread pool so pipeline handlers don't starve
    61→        # FastAPI's default executor (used for sync endpoints).
    62→        self._executor = ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2, thread_name_prefix="outbox")
    63→        self._shutdown_event = asyncio.Event()
    64→
    65→    def poll_and_process(self) -> int:
    66→        """Poll for pending events and process them.
    67→
    68→        Returns:
    69→            Number of events processed in this poll cycle.
    70→        """
    71→        processed = 0
    72→        with Session(self.engine) as session:
    73→            statement = (
    74→                select(OutboxEvent)
    75→                .where(col(OutboxEvent.status).in_(["pending", "failed"]))
    76→                .order_by(OutboxEvent.created_at.asc())  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    77→                .limit(self.batch_size)
    78→            )
    79→
    80→            # Use FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED on PostgreSQL
    81→            # for concurrent processor safety
    82→            db_url = str(self.engine.url)
    83→            if "postgresql" in db_url:
    84→                statement = statement.with_for_update(skip_locked=True)
    85→
    86→            events = session.exec(statement).all()
    87→
    88→            for event in events:
    89→                event_handlers = self.handlers.get(event.event_type, [])
    90→
    91→                if not event_handlers:
    92→                    logger.debug(
    93→                        "No handlers for event type: %s",
    94→                        event.event_type,
    95→                    )
    96→
    97→                try:
    98→                    for handler in event_handlers:
    99→                        handler(event.payload)
   100→
   101→                    event.status = "published"
   102→                    event.published_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
   103→                    session.add(event)
   104→                    processed += 1
   105→
   106→                    logger.info(
   107→                        "Published event %s (type=%s)",
   108→                        event.id,
   109→                        event.event_type,
   110→                    )
   111→                except Exception:
   112→                    event.retry_count += 1
   113→                    if event.retry_count >= MAX_RETRIES:
   114→                        event.status = "dead_letter"
   115→                        logger.warning(
   116→                            "Event %s exhausted retries (%d), marking as dead_letter",
   117→                            event.id,
   118→                            event.retry_count,
   119→                        )
   120→                        # Update protocol status to dead_letter if protocol event
   121→                        if event.aggregate_type == "protocol":
   122→                            from shared.models import Protocol
   123→
   124→                            protocol = session.get(Protocol, event.aggregate_id)
   125→                            if protocol:
   126→                                protocol.status = "dead_letter"
   127→                                protocol.error_reason = "Maximum retries exceeded"
   128→                                protocol.metadata_ = {
   129→                                    **protocol.metadata_,
   130→                                    "error": {
   131→                                        "category": "pipeline_failed",
   132→                                        "reason": "Maximum retries exceeded",
   133→                                        "retry_count": event.retry_count,
   134→                                    },
   135→                                }
   136→                                session.add(protocol)
   137→                    else:
   138→                        event.status = "failed"
   139→
   140→                    session.add(event)
   141→                    logger.exception(
   142→                        "Failed to process event %s (type=%s, retry_count=%d)",
   143→                        event.id,
   144→                        event.event_type,
   145→                        event.retry_count,
   146→                    )
   147→
   148→            session.commit()
   149→
   150→        return processed
   151→
   152→    async def start(self) -> None:
   153→        """Run the polling loop until stop() is called."""
   154→        logger.info(
   155→            "Outbox processor started (interval=%.1fs, batch_size=%d)",
   156→            self.poll_interval,
   157→            self.batch_size,
   158→        )
   159→        while not self._shutdown_event.is_set():
   160→            try:
   161→                # Copy context so MLflow ContextVars propagate to the
   162→                # worker thread, avoiding "created in a different Context"
   163→                # warnings when autologging resets tokens.
   164→                loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   165→                ctx = contextvars.copy_context()
   166→                count = await loop.run_in_executor(
   167→                    self._executor, ctx.run, self.poll_and_process
   168→                )
   169→                if count > 0:
   170→                    logger.info("Processed %d outbox events", count)
   171→            except Exception:
   172→                logger.exception("Error in outbox processor poll cycle")
   173→            try:
   174→                await asyncio.wait_for(
   175→                    self._shutdown_event.wait(),
   176→                    timeout=self.poll_interval,
   177→                )
   178→            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
   179→                pass
   180→        logger.info("Outbox processor stopped")
   181→
   182→    async def stop(self) -> None:
   183→        """Signal the processor to stop after current poll."""
   184→        logger.info("Outbox processor shutdown requested")
   185→        self._shutdown_event.set()
   186→        self._executor.shutdown(wait=True)
   187→
   188→
   189→def persist_with_outbox(
   190→    session: Session,
   191→    entity: Any,
   192→    event_type: DomainEventKind,
   193→    aggregate_type: str,
   194→    aggregate_id: str,
   195→    payload: dict[str, Any],
   196→    idempotency_key: str | None = None,
   197→) -> OutboxEvent:
   198→    """Persist an entity and its outbox event in the same transaction.
   199→
   200→    The caller is responsible for committing the session.
   201→
   202→    Args:
   203→        session: Active SQLModel session (not yet committed).
   204→        entity: The SQLModel entity to persist.
   205→        event_type: Domain event kind.
   206→        aggregate_type: Type of aggregate (e.g. "protocol").
   207→        aggregate_id: ID of the affected aggregate.
   208→        payload: Event payload dict.
   209→        idempotency_key: Dedup key; auto-generated if omitted.
   210→
   211→    Returns:
   212→        The created OutboxEvent (uncommitted).
   213→    """
   214→    ikey = idempotency_key or (f"{event_type.value}:{aggregate_id}:{uuid4()}")
   215→
   216→    outbox_event = OutboxEvent(
   217→        event_type=event_type.value,
   218→        aggregate_type=aggregate_type,
   219→        aggregate_id=aggregate_id,
   220→        payload=payload,
   221→        idempotency_key=ikey,
   222→        status="pending",
   223→    )
   224→
   225→    session.add(entity)
   226→    session.add(outbox_event)
   227→
   228→    return outbox_event
   229→
```

   301→async def retry_from_checkpoint(protocol_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
   302→    """Resume pipeline from last checkpoint for a failed protocol.
   303→
   304→    Passes None as the input state to graph.ainvoke, which tells LangGraph
   305→    to resume from the last saved checkpoint for the given thread_id instead
   306→    of starting from scratch. Reads thread_id from protocol.metadata_ where
   307→    it was stored during the original pipeline invocation.
   308→
   309→    Args:
   310→        protocol_id: Protocol ID — used to look up thread_id from metadata.
   311→
   312→    Returns:
   313→        Final pipeline state dict after resuming from checkpoint.
   314→    """
   315→    with Session(engine) as session:
   316→        protocol = session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   317→        if not protocol:
   318→            raise ValueError(f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found")
   319→        thread_id = (protocol.metadata_ or {}).get("pipeline_thread_id", protocol_id)
   320→
   321→    from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph
   322→
   323→    graph = await get_graph()
   324→    config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": thread_id}}
   325→    # Pass None as input — LangGraph resumes from last checkpoint
   326→    result = await graph.ainvoke(None, config)
   327→    return result
   328→

> AGENT

Found it. Line 279: `asyncio.run(_run_pipeline(...))`. The problem is that `poll_and_process` runs in a `ThreadPoolExecutor` thread (line 166-167 in outbox.py), which calls `handler(event.payload)`, which calls `asyncio.run()`. But `asyncio.run()` creates a new event loop — this can fail if there's already an event loop running in the parent thread.

Actually, looking more carefully: the outbox runs `poll_and_process` in a dedicated `ThreadPoolExecutor` (line 62, 166-167), which is a separate thread without an event loop. So `asyncio.run()` should work. 

Let me check if the outbox processor is actually running and picking up events. Let me look at the uvicorn logs.

> AGENT

There's the problem. Multiple stale transactions:

- **PID 70952**: "idle in transaction" for 3h48m — old outbox SELECT with FOR UPDATE lock
- **PID 73349**: DELETE from outboxevent blocked for 3h21m (waiting on the lock)
- **PID 73458, 71062, 79802, 80956**: Stuck DELETE/UPDATE from criteria (cascade locks)
- **PID 88738**: Current outbox processor also "idle in transaction" — locked waiting on the old row locks

The old killed uvicorn left stale PostgreSQL connections. I need to terminate these to unblock everything.

The handler is calling `asyncio.run()` inside the outbox processor's thread pool. But since the handler runs synchronously and calls `asyncio.run()`, this creates a new event loop. The problem is clear: `asyncio.run()` on line 279 creates a new event loop. But the outbox processor runs `poll_and_process` in a `ThreadPoolExecutor` using `ctx.run` (contextvars copy). The `asyncio.run()` should work fine in a thread pool thread since there's no existing event loop in that thread.

But wait — the `pipeline_thread_id` was stored successfully (we can see it in metadata). So the code reached line 279 but either `asyncio.run()` silently failed or the pipeline raised an exception that was caught on line 286 and the protocol was marked as failed.      1→"""Ingest node: fetch PDF bytes from GCS or local storage.
     2→
     3→This node is the first in the protocol processing graph. It fetches the PDF
     4→from the file_uri (local:// or gs://) and populates state with raw PDF bytes.
     5→
     6→Architecture note: Graph nodes are integration glue and ARE allowed to import
     7→from api-service for database access (e.g., api_service.storage.engine).
     8→This cross-service import is intentional for workflow orchestration nodes.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→from typing import Any
    15→
    16→from api_service.storage import engine
    17→from shared.models import Protocol
    18→from sqlmodel import Session
    19→
    20→from protocol_processor.state import PipelineState
    21→from protocol_processor.tools.pdf_parser import fetch_pdf_bytes
    22→from protocol_processor.tracing import pipeline_span
    23→
    24→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    25→
    26→
    27→async def ingest_node(state: PipelineState) -> dict[str, Any]:
    28→    """Fetch PDF bytes and update protocol status to 'extracting'.
    29→
    30→    Delegates PDF fetching to the fetch_pdf_bytes tool. Updates the
    31→    protocol record status to 'extracting' in the database.
    32→
    33→    Args:
    34→        state: Current pipeline state with protocol_id, file_uri, and title.
    35→
    36→    Returns:
    37→        Dict with pdf_bytes and status="processing", or error dict on failure.
    38→    """
    39→    if state.get("error"):
    40→        return {}
    41→
    42→    with pipeline_span("ingest_node", protocol_id=state.get("protocol_id", "")) as span:
    43→        span.set_inputs(
    44→            {
    45→                "protocol_id": state.get("protocol_id", ""),
    46→                "file_uri": state.get("file_uri", ""),
    47→            }
    48→        )
    49→
    50→        try:
    51→            pdf_bytes = await fetch_pdf_bytes(state["file_uri"])
    52→
    53→            # Update protocol status to 'extracting'
    54→            with Session(engine) as session:
    55→                protocol = session.get(Protocol, state["protocol_id"])
    56→                if protocol:
    57→                    protocol.status = "extracting"
    58→                    session.add(protocol)
    59→                    session.commit()
    60→
    61→            logger.info(
    62→                "Ingested protocol %s: %d bytes of PDF",
    63→                state["protocol_id"],
    64→                len(pdf_bytes),
    65→            )
    66→            span.set_outputs({"pdf_bytes_len": len(pdf_bytes), "status": "processing"})
    67→            return {"pdf_bytes": pdf_bytes, "status": "processing"}
    68→
    69→        except Exception as e:
    70→            logger.exception(
    71→                "Ingestion failed for protocol %s: %s",
    72→                state.get("protocol_id", "unknown"),
    73→                e,
    74→            )
    75→            span.set_outputs({"error": str(e)})
    76→            return {"error": f"Ingestion failed: {e}", "status": "failed"}
    77→
```

> AGENT

When ingest fails, it returns `{"error": ..., "status": "failed"}` but does NOT update the database status — so the protocol stays "uploaded". And the graph sees the `error` in state and likely routes to END, so the handler returns without exception and the outbox marks it "published".

The actual problem is: **the ingest node couldn't find the file when run from the outbox processor thread**. The CWD might be different, or the `LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` is resolving differently inside the thread vs the main uvicorn process.

Let me verify by checking the `fetch_pdf_bytes` path resolution.

> AGENT

Line 77-80: `Path("uploads/protocols").resolve()` — this resolves relative to CWD. When the outbox processor thread runs, the CWD should be the same as the main process (repo root). 

The issue is the outbox processor ran in the thread but the handler's `asyncio.run()` seems to have silently swallowed the error. Looking again at trigger.py line 286-298, the exception handler calls `_update_protocol_failed` and then re-raises. But the outbox processor catches it on line 111 and marks it as failed.

Wait, but the event was marked "published" not "failed"! This means the handler returned without raising. The `_run_pipeline` returned normally despite ingest failing, because the graph's error handling sets `state["error"]` and continues to END.

So the pipeline ran, ingest failed silently (returned error state), and the handler returned "success" to the outbox. The protocol stays "uploaded" because ingest didn't update the status on failure.

This is actually the correct behavior of the first protocol (74366047) that completed — it must have had the file in the right place (uploaded from the old uvicorn instance which had `data/uploads` mounted as `uploads/protocols` in Docker, or was the same process).

The fix is simple: ensure the upload directory is consistent. Let me check where the latest file actually was saved to.

> AGENT

Found the bug. The dedup symlink uses a relative path `uploads/protocols/4b70fabd.../...` which is relative to the **CWD**, not to the symlink's location. When resolved from the symlink's directory, it tries to find `uploads/protocols/e65d9a1e.../uploads/protocols/4b70fabd.../...` which doesn't exist.

This is a bug in the `local_save_file` dedup logic in `gcs.py`. But for our smoketest, I'll just use the first uploaded protocol (4b70fabd) directly, or upload a fresh file. Actually the simplest fix: let me upload a new PDF that won't hit the dedup path.

Here's the situation:

**What happened:**
1. Upload + extraction worked fine (uploaded → extracting in ~5s, extraction completed in ~20s)
2. Pipeline entered grounding and has been stuck for 30+ minutes with 0 entities processed
3. The MedGemma Vertex AI endpoint (`VERTEX_ENDPOINT_ID`) appears completely unresponsive — even the GCP API call to check the endpoint status timed out

**Root cause:** The MedGemma Vertex endpoint (`min_replica_count=0`) is either:
- Still cold-starting (unlikely after 30+ min, cold-start is typically ~5 min)
- The endpoint has been shut down / undeployed
- Network/auth issue reaching `europe-west4`

**Additional issues found during this smoketest:**
1. **Dedup symlink bug** in `api_service.gcs.local_save_file` — creates relative symlinks that don't resolve correctly (caused first test failure)
2. **Stale DB connections** — killed uvicorn processes leave PostgreSQL locks behind, blocking the outbox processor
3. **Silent ingest failures** — when ingest fails, protocol stays "uploaded" (no DB status update on error)

**What I recommend:**
1. Check if the MedGemma Vertex endpoint is still deployed on GCP console
2. For the smoketest, we could skip MedGemma by setting `USE_AI_GROUNDING=false` to test the non-MedGemma path (regex fallback)
3. Or test just extraction (which worked) by stopping after the parse node

- **B)** Kill the stuck pipeline and check the Vertex endpoint status from the GCP console?
